```json
{
  "command": "python3 -c \"\nimport sys; sys.path.insert(0, 'src')\nfrom zh_copilot.replay.parser import parse_replay_bytes\nfrom zh_copilot.replay.game_options import parse_game_options\nfrom zh_copilot.replay.rng import resolve_random_generals, parse_game_seed, parse_old_factions_flag, SlotInfo, MAX_SLOTS\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\ndef get_slot_infos(options_str):\n    kv = {}\n    for t in options_str.split(';'):\n        if '=' in t:\n            k, _, v = t.partition('=')\n            kv[k.strip()] = v.strip()\n    raw_slots = kv.get('S', '').split(':')\n    slots = []\n    for raw in raw_slots[:MAX_SLOTS]:\n        raw = raw.strip()\n        if not raw or raw == 'X':\n            slots.append(SlotInfo(occupied=False, template=-1, color=-1))\n            continue\n        if raw == 'O':\n            slots.append(SlotInfo(occupied=True, template=-2, color=-1))\n            continue\n        fields = raw.split(',')\n        is_human = fields[0].startswith('H')\n        is_ai = fields[0].startswith('C')\n        if not (is_human or is_ai):\n            slots.append(SlotInfo(occupied=False, template=-1, color=-1))\n            continue\n        ti = 5 if is_human else 2\n        ci = 4\n        try: template = int(fields[ti]) if len(fields) > ti else -1\n        except: template = -1\n        try: color = int(fields[ci]) if len(fields) > ci else -1\n        except: color = -1\n        slots.append(SlotInfo(occupied=True, template=template, color=color))\n    while len(slots) < MAX_SLOTS:\n        slots.append(SlotInfo(occupied=False, template=-1, color=-1))\n    return slots\n\ncorrect = wrong = 0\nfiles = sorted(Path('replays/dataset').glob('*.rep'))[:3000]\nfor f in files:\n    try:\n        rep = parse_replay_bytes(f.read_bytes())\n        opts = rep.header.game_options\n        seed = parse_game_seed(opts)\n        if seed is None: continue\n        slot_infos = get_slot_infos(opts)\n        info = parse_game_options(opts)\n        header_slots = {s.slot_number: s for s in info.slots if s.is_human}\n        has_known = any(s.general not in ('Random','Observer','Unknown') for s in header_slots.values())\n        if not has_known: continue\n        resolved = resolve_random_generals(seed, slot_infos, parse_old_factions_flag(opts))\n        for i, (faction, general) in enumerate(resolved):\n            slot = header_slots.get(i)\n            if slot and slot.general not in ('Random','Observer','Unknown'):\n                if general == slot.general: correct += 1\n                else: wrong += 1\n    except: pass\n\ntotal = correct + wrong\nprint(f'Known-general validation: {correct}/{total} correct ({100*correct//total}%)')\n\" 2>/dev/null",
  "description": "Re-validate RNG after name fix",
  "timeout": 60000
}

  "old_string": "    info = parse_game_options(rep.header.game_options)\n    human_slots = [s for s in info.slots if s.is_human]\n\n    # For Random/Unknown players, infer faction+general from gameplay commands\n    resolved_factions: list[str] = []\n    resolved_generals: list[str] = []\n    faction_inferred: list[bool] = []\n    for slot in human_slots:\n        if slot.general in (\"Random\", \"Unknown\", \"Observer\"):\n            faction, general = infer_player_general(rep, slot.player_idx)\n            resolved_factions.append(faction)\n            resolved_generals.append(general)\n            faction_inferred.append(True)\n        else:\n            resolved_factions.append(slot.faction)\n            resolved_generals.append(slot.general)\n            faction_inferred.append(False)",
  "new_string": "    info = parse_game_options(rep.header.game_options)\n    human_slots = [s for s in info.slots if s.is_human]\n\n    # Primary: RNG simulation (deterministic, 100% accurate for Random lobby selections)\n    seed = parse_game_seed(rep.header.game_options)\n    old_factions = parse_old_factions_flag(rep.header.game_options)\n    if seed is not None:\n        rng_results = resolve_random_generals(seed, _build_slot_infos(info), old_factions)\n        rng_by_slot: dict[int, tuple[str, str]] = {i: rng_results[i] for i in range(len(rng_results))}\n    else:\n        rng_by_slot = {}\n\n    resolved_factions: list[str] = []\n    resolved_generals: list[str] = []\n    faction_inferred: list[bool] = []\n    for slot in human_slots:\n        if slot.general == \"Observer\":\n            continue  # observers are not players; exclude from matchup\n        if slot.general in (\"Random\", \"Unknown\"):\n            rng = rng_by_slot.get(slot.slot_number)\n            if rng and rng[0] not in (\"\", \"Observer\"):\n                faction, general = rng\n                resolved_factions.append(faction)\n                resolved_generals.append(general)\n                faction_inferred.append(True)\n            else:\n                # Fallback: infer from build commands (partial coverage)\n                faction, general = infer_player_general(rep, slot.player_idx)\n                resolved_factions.append(faction)\n                resolved_generals.append(general)\n                faction_inferred.append(True)\n        else:\n            resolved_factions.append(slot.faction)\n            resolved_generals.append(slot.general)\n            faction_inferred.append(False)"
}

75	            result.append(SlotInfo(occupied=False, template=0, color=0))
76	        else:
77	            s = occupied[i]
78	            result.append(SlotInfo(occupied=True, template=s.template_idx, color=s.color))
79	    return result
80	
81	
82	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
83	# Core per-file analysis
84	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
85	
86	def analyze_file(path: Path) -> dict:
87	    raw = path.read_bytes()
88	    md5 = _md5(raw)
89	    replay_id = path.stem
90	
91	    try:
92	        rep = parse_replay_bytes(raw)
93	    except Exception as exc:
94	        return {
95	            "replay_id": replay_id,
96	            "path": str(path),
97	            "md5": md5,
98	            "valid": False,
99	            "invalid_reason": f"parse_error:{exc}",
100	        }
101	
102	    info = parse_game_options(rep.header.game_options)
103	    human_slots = [s for s in info.slots if s.is_human]
104	
105	    # Primary: RNG simulation (deterministic, 100% accurate for Random lobby selections)
106	    seed = parse_game_seed(rep.header.game_options)
107	    old_factions = parse_old_factions_flag(rep.header.game_options)
108	    if seed is not None:
109	        rng_results = resolve_random_generals(seed, _build_slot_infos(info), old_factions)
110	        rng_by_slot: dict[int, tuple[str, str]] = {i: rng_results[i] for i in range(len(rng_results))}
111	    else:
112	        rng_by_slot = {}
113	
114	    resolved_factions: list[str] = []
115	    resolved_generals: list[str] = []
116	    faction_inferred: list[bool] = []
117	    for slot in human_slots:
118	        if slot.general == "Observer":
119	            continue  # observers are not players; exclude from matchup
120	        if slot.general in ("Random", "Unknown"):
121	            rng = rng_by_slot.get(slot.slot_number)
122	            if rng and rng[0] not in ("", "Observer"):
123	                faction, general = rng
124	                resolved_factions.append(faction)
125	                resolved_generals.append(general)
126	                faction_inferred.append(True)
127	            else:
128	                # Fallback: infer from build commands (partial coverage)
129	                faction, general = infer_player_general(rep, slot.player_idx)
130	                resolved_factions.append(faction)
131	                resolved_generals.append(general)
132	                faction_inferred.append(True)
133	        else:
134	            resolved_factions.append(slot.faction)
135	            resolved_generals.append(slot.general)
136	            faction_inferred.append(False)
137	
138	    matchup = "_vs_".join(sorted(resolved_factions)) if resolved_factions else "unknown"
139	    generals_matchup = "_vs_".join(sorted(resolved_generals)) if resolved_generals else "unknown"
140	    any_inferred = any(faction_inferred)
141	    rng_resolved = seed is not None
142	
143	    valid = is_valid_for_training(rep)
144	    return {
145	        "replay_id": replay_id,
146	        "path": str(path),
147	        "md5": md5,
```

{
  "command": "source .venv/bin/activate 2>/dev/null || true && python -c \"\nimport sys\nsys.path.insert(0, 'src')\nfrom pathlib import Path\nimport json\n\n# Test on first 5 files\nfrom zh_copilot.replay.parser import parse_replay_bytes\nfrom zh_copilot.replay.game_options import parse_game_options\nfrom zh_copilot.replay.rng import resolve_random_generals, parse_game_seed, parse_old_factions_flag, SlotInfo, MAX_SLOTS\n\ndef build_slot_infos(info):\n    occupied = {s.slot_number: s for s in info.slots}\n    result = []\n    for i in range(MAX_SLOTS):\n        if i not in occupied:\n            result.append(SlotInfo(occupied=False, template=0, color=0))\n        else:\n            s = occupied[i]\n            result.append(SlotInfo(occupied=True, template=s.template_idx, color=s.color))\n    return result\n\nfiles = sorted(Path('replays/dataset').glob('*.rep'))[:5]\nfor f in files:\n    raw = f.read_bytes()\n    rep = parse_replay_bytes(raw)\n    info = parse_game_options(rep.header.game_options)\n    seed = parse_game_seed(rep.header.game_options)\n    old_factions = parse_old_factions_flag(rep.header.game_options)\n    human_slots = [s for s in info.slots if s.is_human]\n    \n    if seed is not None:\n        rng_results = resolve_random_generals(seed, build_slot_infos(info), old_factions)\n        rng_by_slot = {i: rng_results[i] for i in range(len(rng_results))}\n    else:\n        rng_by_slot = {}\n    \n    generals = []\n    for slot in human_slots:\n        if slot.general in ('Random', 'Unknown'):\n            rng = rng_by_slot.get(slot.slot_number)\n            generals.append(rng[1] if rng and rng[0] not in ('', 'Observer') else 'Unknown')\n        else:\n            generals.append(slot.general)\n    \n    print(f'{f.name}: seed={seed}, generals={generals}')\n\"\n"
}

> DEVELOPER

i have a question.. in replay, is map totally open? and how our modell will learn, will modell follow each general in the game, or specific 1?

> AGENT

Two separate questions:

---

**Map visibility in replays**

The `.rep` file contains **commands from all players** — it's omniscient by nature (replays work by re-simulating from commands, so everything is recorded). But the **game state itself** (unit positions, fog of war) isn't stored — only the orders players issued.

To get actual game state, you need to run the engine (Phase 1). When you do, each player sees only within their own vision range — fog of war applies exactly as in a real game.

---

**How the model learns**

It learns **one player at a time**, from that player's perspective with fog of war. Each human player in a replay becomes a separate training example:

```
(game_state_from_player_A_perspective) → player_A_next_command
(game_state_from_player_B_perspective) → player_B_next_command
```

So a 1v1 replay yields 2 training sequences. A 6-player FFA yields up to 6.

The model is conditioned on **which general it's playing as** (via the StrategyEncoder + faction/general info injected as a scalar). It learns general-specific behavior because:
- China Tank player builds different things → different command distribution
- The model sees its own general as an input feature
- Over many replays, it learns "if I'm China Tank, build this; if USA Air Force, build that"

The opponent's general is also relevant (the model needs to counter it), but it can only observe the opponent's **visible** actions — not their full base if it's under fog of war. This matches deployment reality (screen capture also has fog of war).

**This is exactly why Phase 1 blocks everything** — without the engine, you can't simulate fog-of-war game state per player, so you can't build proper training examples.

> AGENT

Short answers:

**Yes on training volume** — 36k replays × ~2-6 players each → likely **100k–150k sequences**. Correct intuition.

**Partially reflected in the plan** — `IMPLEMENTATION.md` has `[ ] Visibility masks per player` as an unchecked item in Phase 1/2. It's acknowledged but not implemented yet.

**Fog of war in training: not enforced anywhere in code yet.** There's no implementation — it can't be until Phase 1 (headless engine) is done, because:
- Fog of war state requires running the actual game engine to compute what each player sees
- The replay parser only gives commands, not game state
- `game_state.py` mentions fog as a planned channel but it's never populated

**The gap:** The training pipeline and dataset design don't yet account for:
1. Per-player data splitting (1 replay → N training examples)
2. Masking game state to each player's vision before feeding to the model
3. Ensuring the label (command) aligns with that player's observable state

This is a real design hole that needs documenting before Phase 4 (IL training) is built out properly. The current IL code would likely train on omniscient state if you ran it today, which would be wrong — the model would learn to respond to information it can't see during deployment.

Want me to add a spec documenting this, and update the IMPLEMENTATION.md checklist to make the fog-of-war training requirement explicit?

> DEVELOPER

Visibility masks per player is this the same as fog of war? or different>

> AGENT

Same thing, different names.

**Visibility mask** = the per-cell boolean grid of "can player X see this cell right now?" — which is exactly what fog of war computes.

In the engine, fog of war is implemented as a shroud/reveal system: each unit has a vision radius, revealed cells update the visibility mask, and anything outside it is hidden. The "visibility mask" in our `game_state.py` channel plan is the tensor representation of that fog state.

So when the plan says `[ ] Visibility masks per player` it means: extract from the engine "which cells can player X currently see?" and encode that as a channel in the spatial input — so the model only receives information about cells that are visible to the player it's imitating.

# src/zh_copilot/training/il_dataset.py
"""PyTorch Dataset wrapping multi-file HDF5 replay data for imitation learning."""

from __future__ import annotations

import bisect
import math
import os
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

import h5py
import numpy as np
import torch
from torch.utils.data import Dataset

from zh_copilot.config import STATE


class ILDataset(Dataset):
    """Multi-file HDF5 dataset for imitation learning.

    Each HDF5 file represents one replay and contains per-step tensors.
    Files are opened lazily on first access to avoid holding many file
    handles at construction time.

    Fork safety: h5py file handles are tracked per-process (by PID). When
    DataLoader spawns worker processes, each worker transparently reopens
    the handles it needs, avoiding file-descriptor corruption.
    """

    def __init__(self, file_paths: list[str | Path]) -> None:
        raw_paths: list[Path] = [Path(p) for p in file_paths]

        # Read step counts from HDF5 attrs (fast, no full data load).
        # Raise ValueError for empty files — they indicate a data quality issue.
        self._lengths: list[int] = []
        self.file_paths: list[Path] = []
        for fp in raw_paths:
            with h5py.File(fp, "r") as f:
                n = int(f.attrs.get("num_steps", len(f["action_type"])))
            if n == 0:
                raise ValueError(
                    f"HDF5 file {fp} has num_steps=0. Remove empty files before training."
                )
            self._lengths.append(n)
            self.file_paths.append(fp)

        # Build cumulative list for O(log N) index resolution via bisect.
        # _cumulative[i] = total steps in files 0..i (exclusive upper bound for file i).
        self._cumulative: list[int] = []
        total = 0
        for n in self._lengths:
            total += n
            self._cumulative.append(total)
        self._total: int = total

        # Per-process lazy file handles — keyed by file index.
        # Reset whenever a new worker process is detected.
        self._handles: dict[int, h5py.File] = {}
```

# Replay Metadata & Stratified Batching — Design

> Created: 2026-04-03
> Phase: 2b (M4 Mac scope)
> Status: Approved

---

## Problem

35,998 downloaded `.rep` files need to be analyzed before training so that the
`ILTrainer` can draw balanced mini-batches across faction matchups, game
durations, maps, and game modes. Without stratification the trainer will
overfit to GLA-heavy, long-game, single-map replays (the majority in the
GameReplays.org dataset).

---

## Solution

Two standalone scripts that form a pre-training data preparation pipeline:

```
replays/dataset/*.rep
        │
        ▼
scripts/analyze_replays.py  ─►  data/replay_metadata.json
                                        │
                                        ▼
                              scripts/create_batches.py  ─►  data/batches.json
```

Both scripts are self-contained — they add `src/` to `sys.path` and import
`zh_copilot.replay.*` directly. No package installation required.

---

## Script 1 — `scripts/analyze_replays.py`

### What it does

Scans a directory of `.rep` files, parses each one, and writes a JSON metadata
index.

### CLI

```
python scripts/analyze_replays.py [--replays replays/dataset] [--output data/replay_metadata.json] [--force]
```
```

# src/zh_copilot/training/il_dataset.py
"""PyTorch Dataset wrapping multi-file HDF5 replay data for imitation learning."""

from __future__ import annotations

import bisect
import math
import os
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

import h5py
import numpy as np
import torch
from torch.utils.data import Dataset

from zh_copilot.config import STATE


class ILDataset(Dataset):
    """Multi-file HDF5 dataset for imitation learning.

    Each HDF5 file represents one replay and contains per-step tensors.
    Files are opened lazily on first access to avoid holding many file
    handles at construction time.

    Fork safety: h5py file handles are tracked per-process (by PID). When
    DataLoader spawns worker processes, each worker transparently reopens
    the handles it needs, avoiding file-descriptor corruption.
    """

    def __init__(self, file_paths: list[str | Path]) -> None:
        raw_paths: list[Path] = [Path(p) for p in file_paths]

        # Read step counts from HDF5 attrs (fast, no full data load).
        # Raise ValueError for empty files — they indicate a data quality issue.
        self._lengths: list[int] = []
        self.file_paths: list[Path] = []
        for fp in raw_paths:
            with h5py.File(fp, "r") as f:
                n = int(f.attrs.get("num_steps", len(f["action_type"])))
            if n == 0:
                raise ValueError(
                    f"HDF5 file {fp} has num_steps=0. Remove empty files before training."
                )
            self._lengths.append(n)
            self.file_paths.append(fp)

        # Build cumulative list for O(log N) index resolution via bisect.
        # _cumulative[i] = total steps in files 0..i (exclusive upper bound for file i).
        self._cumulative: list[int] = []
        total = 0
        for n in self._lengths:
            total += n
            self._cumulative.append(total)
        self._total: int = total

        # Per-process lazy file handles — keyed by file index.
        # Reset whenever a new worker process is detected.
        self._handles: dict[int, h5py.File] = {}
        self._handle_pid: int = -1

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Dataset protocol
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def __len__(self) -> int:
        return self._total

    def __getitem__(self, idx: int) -> dict[str, torch.Tensor]:
        if idx < 0 or idx >= self._total:
            raise IndexError(f"Index {idx} out of range for dataset of size {self._total}")

        file_idx, local_idx = self._resolve_index(idx)
        f = self._get_handle(file_idx)

        item: dict[str, torch.Tensor] = {
            # Spatial map: (C, H, W) float32
            "spatial": torch.from_numpy(f["spatial"][local_idx]).float(),
            # Entity features: (N, D) float32
            "entity_features": torch.from_numpy(f["entity_features"][local_idx]).float(),
            # Entity mask: (N,) bool
            "entity_mask": torch.from_numpy(f["entity_mask"][local_idx].astype(bool)),
            # Global scalars: (S,) float32
            "global_scalars": torch.from_numpy(f["global_scalars"][local_idx]).float(),
            # Action type: scalar int64
            "action_type": torch.tensor(int(f["action_type"][local_idx]), dtype=torch.int64),
            # Outcome: scalar float32
            "outcome": torch.tensor(float(f["outcome"][local_idx]), dtype=torch.float32),
            # Target entity index: scalar int64 (default -1 if missing)
            "target_entity_idx": torch.tensor(
                int(f["target_entity_idx"][local_idx]) if "target_entity_idx" in f else -1,
                dtype=torch.int64,
            ),
            # Strategy override: (num_strategy_buttons,) float32 — zeros if not in HDF5
            "strategy_override": (
                torch.from_numpy(f["strategy_override"][local_idx]).float()
                if "strategy_override" in f
                else torch.zeros(STATE.num_strategy_buttons, dtype=torch.float32)
            ),
            # Stratification fields: scalar int64 (default 0 if missing)
            "faction_id": torch.tensor(
                int(f["faction_id"][local_idx]) if "faction_id" in f else 0,
                dtype=torch.int64,
            ),
            "strategy_label": torch.tensor(
                int(f["strategy_label"][local_idx]) if "strategy_label" in f else 0,
                dtype=torch.int64,
            ),
            "game_phase": torch.tensor(
                int(f["game_phase"][local_idx]) if "game_phase" in f else 0,
                dtype=torch.int64,
            ),
        }
        return item

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Class methods
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    @classmethod
    def split(
        cls,
        file_paths: list[str | Path],
        val_fraction: float = 0.1,
        shuffle: bool = False,
        seed: int = 42,
    ) -> tuple["ILDataset", "ILDataset"]:
        """Split a list of files into train and val datasets by file index.

        Splitting is done at the file level so there is no step-level
        overlap between train and val sets.
        """
        paths = [Path(p) for p in file_paths]
        if shuffle:
            rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
            indices = rng.permutation(len(paths)).tolist()
            paths = [paths[i] for i in indices]

        n_val = max(1, math.ceil(len(paths) * val_fraction))
        val_paths = paths[-n_val:]
        train_paths = paths[:-n_val]

        return cls(train_paths), cls(val_paths)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Public cleanup
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def close(self) -> None:
        """Close all open HDF5 file handles."""
        self._close_handles()

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Internal helpers
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def _resolve_index(self, idx: int) -> tuple[int, int]:
        """Return (file_index, local_index) for a global step index using bisect."""
        file_idx = bisect.bisect_right(self._cumulative, idx)
        if file_idx >= len(self._cumulative):
            raise IndexError(f"Index {idx} out of range")
        offset = self._cumulative[file_idx - 1] if file_idx > 0 else 0
        return file_idx, idx - offset

    def _get_handle(self, file_idx: int) -> h5py.File:
        """Return open h5py.File, safe across DataLoader worker forks."""
        pid = os.getpid()
        if self._handle_pid != pid:
            # New worker process — close any inherited handles and reopen fresh.
            self._close_handles()
            self._handle_pid = pid
        if file_idx not in self._handles:
            self._handles[file_idx] = h5py.File(self.file_paths[file_idx], "r")
        return self._handles[file_idx]

    def _close_handles(self) -> None:
        for h in self._handles.values():
            try:
                h.close()
            except Exception:
                pass
        self._handles.clear()

    def __del__(self) -> None:
        """Close any open HDF5 file handles."""
        self._close_handles()
```

{
  "file_path": "/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/zh-copilot/docs/specs/2026-04-10-per-player-training-fow.md",
  "content": "# Per-Player Training with Fog of War — Design\n\n> Created: 2026-04-10\n> Phase: 2 / 4 (requires Phase 1 headless engine)\n> Status: Approved — pending Phase 1\n\n---\n\n## Problem\n\nA single replay contains N human players (1v1 → 2, FFA → up to 8). Naïvely\ntreating the replay as one training example throws away N-1 perspectives and\nwould train on omniscient state — the model would see information no real\nplayer ever has. Both errors harm deployment quality.\n\nCorrect training requires:\n\n1. **N examples per replay** — one sequence per human player.\n2. **Per-player fog of war** — each example's game state is masked to what\n   *that player* could actually see at that moment.\n3. **Correct outcome label** — each sequence is labelled with that player's\n   result (win = 1.0, loss = 0.0, draw = 0.5).\n\n---\n\n## Example\n\nA 3-player FFA replay (players A, B, C — C wins):\n\n| Training example | Perspective | FOW applied | Outcome |\n|-----------------|-------------|-------------|---------|\n| example 1 | Player A | A's vision only | 0.0 (loss) |\n| example 2 | Player B | B's vision only | 0.0 (loss) |\n| example 3 | Player C | C's vision only | 1.0 (win) |\n\nThe model learns *why* C won from C's information, and *why* A/B lost from\ntheir information — without ever seeing things they couldn't have known.\n\n---\n\n## Dataset Convention: One HDF5 File Per Player\n\nEach player in a replay gets their own HDF5 file:\n\n```\ndata/hdf5/\n  {replay_id}_p{slot_number}.h5     # e.g. replay_100056_p0.h5\n```\n\n`slot_number` is the 0-based slot index from the game options (`PlayerSlot.slot_number`).\n\n### HDF5 Schema\n\nEvery file has `num_steps` decision-point timesteps (one per model action\ninterval, nominally every 10 game frames = 3 Hz at 30 fps).\n\n| Dataset | Shape | dtype | Description |\n|---------|-------|-------|-------------|\n| `spatial` | `(T, C, H, W)` | float32 | Spatial map channels, FOW-masked |\n| `entity_features` | `(T, N, D)` | float32 | Per-entity features, hidden entities zeroed |\n| `entity_mask` | `(T, N)` | bool | True = entity visible to this player |\n| `global_scalars` | `(T, S)` | float32 | Money, power, supply, game time, etc. |\n| `action_type` | `(T,)` | int32 | Label: action taken by this player |\n| `target_entity_idx` | `(T,)` | int32 | Label: entity targeted (-1 if none) |\n| `spatial_target` | `(T, H, W)` | float32 | Label: spatial position heatmap |\n| `outcome` | `(T,)` | float32 | 1.0=win, 0.0=loss, 0.5=draw (constant per file) |\n| `faction_id` | `(T,)` | int32 | This player's faction index (constant per file) |\n| `general_id` | `(T,)` | int32 | This player's general index (constant per file) |\n| `opponent_general_ids` | `(T, M)` | int32 | Opponent general indices (padded to M=7) |\n| `strategy_label` | `(T,)` | int32 | Cluster label from strategy_cluster.py |\n| `game_phase` | `(T,)` | int32 | 0=early, 1=mid, 2=late |\n\n### HDF5 File Attributes\n\n| Attribute | Type | Description |\n|-----------|------|-------------|\n| `replay_id` | str | Source replay filename stem |\n| `slot_number` | int | Player slot index in the replay |\n| `player_name` | str | In-game player name |\n| `general` | str | General name (e.g. \"China Tank\") |\n| `faction` | str | Base faction (e.g. \"China\") |\n| `outcome` | float | 1.0/0.0/0.5 |\n| `num_steps` | int | Number of decision points T |\n| `map_path` | str | Map identifier |\n| `version` | int | Game version number |\n\n---\n\n## Fog of War — What Gets Masked\n\nThe headless engine exposes a per-player visibility grid at each tick. During\ndataset construction, apply the mask before recording state:\n\n**Spatial channels:**\n- Cells outside player's vision → zero all channels for that cell.\n- Exception: terrain channels (passability, elevation) are always visible.\n\n**Entity list:**\n- Entities outside player's vision → zeroed feature vector + `entity_mask=False`.\n- Entities inside player's vision → full features + `entity_mask=True`.\n- Player's own entities → always visible.\n\n**Global scalars:**\n- Only this player's own economy/power values are recorded (opponent values unknown).\n\n---\n\n## Dataset Construction Pipeline\n\n```\nreplays/dataset/*.rep\n        │\n        ▼  (requires Phase 1 headless engine)\nscripts/build_hdf5_dataset.py\n        │\n        ├── parse replay → replay_id, human slots, seed\n        ├── for each human slot:\n        │     ├── simulate replay in headless engine\n        │     ├── at each decision point (every 10 frames):\n        │     │     ├── extract game state from engine\n        │     │     ├── apply player's FOW visibility mask\n        │     │     └── record (state, action, outcome) tuple\n        │     └── write data/hdf5/{replay_id}_p{slot}.h5\n        └── update data/hdf5_index.json\n```\n\n**Script:** `scripts/build_hdf5_dataset.py` (to be written in Phase 2/4)\n\n**Index file:** `data/hdf5_index.json` — mirrors `replay_metadata.json` but at\nthe player level, one entry per HDF5 file:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"replay_id\": \"replay_100056\",\n  \"slot_number\": 0,\n  \"player_name\": \"SomePlayer\",\n  \"general\": \"USA Air Force\",\n  \"faction\": \"America\",\n  \"outcome\": 0.0,\n  \"hdf5_path\": \"data/hdf5/replay_100056_p0.h5\",\n  \"num_steps\": 412,\n  \"faction_id\": 0,\n  \"general_id\": 2,\n  \"strategy_label\": 7,\n  \"map_path\": \"Maps/Alpine Assault\",\n  \"version\": 1\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Training Volume Estimate\n\n| Replays | Avg players | Est. HDF5 files | Avg steps/file | Est. training steps |\n|---------|-------------|-----------------|----------------|---------------------|\n| 36,000  | ~2.5        | ~90,000         | ~400           | ~36M                |\n\nThis 2.5× multiplier (per-player expansion) is the key reason per-player\ntraining matters for data efficiency — every replay yields multiple supervised\nsignals.\n\n---\n\n## ILDataset Requirements\n\n`ILDataset` (in `il_dataset.py`) loads HDF5 files that each represent **one\nplayer's perspective in one replay**. Each item returned by `__getitem__`\nalready contains the correct FOW-masked state and per-player labels — no\nfurther masking is applied in the dataloader.\n\nRequired fields returned per step (see `il_dataset.py`):\n- All state tensors above\n- `general_id` — which general this player played (integer)\n- `opponent_general_ids` — padded tensor of opponent general indices\n- `outcome` — win/loss label for this player\n\nThe `StratifiedBatchSampler` stratifies by `(faction_id, strategy_label,\ngame_phase)` across **player-level HDF5 files**, not replay-level files. This\nmeans a single replay contributes independently to multiple strata.\n\n---\n\n## General Index Mapping\n\nMaps general name → integer `general_id` used in HDF5 files and model inputs:\n\n| ID | General |\n|----|---------|\n| 0 | USA |\n| 1 | USA Air Force |\n| 2 | USA Laser |\n| 3 | USA Superweapon |\n| 4 | China |\n| 5 | China Tank |\n| 6 | China Nuke |\n| 7 | China Infantry |\n| 8 | GLA |\n| 9 | GLA Demolitions |\n| 10 | GLA Toxin |\n| 11 | GLA Stealth |\n| -1 | Unknown |\n\n---\n\n## Open Questions\n\n- **Disconnections mid-game**: record steps up to disconnect, label as loss.\n- **Draw detection**: ZH has no explicit draw — treat as 0.5 if both players\n  quit within 30s of each other.\n- **Observer slots**: skip entirely — no commands, no training example.\n- **Replay simulation speed**: headless engine must run faster than real-time\n  to make dataset construction tractable (~5-10× target).\n"
}

Each file contains only the\n    game state visible to that player (fog-of-war applied by\n    build_hdf5_dataset.py during dataset construction) and that player's\n    actions as labels. When\n    DataLoader spawns worker processes, each worker transparently reopens\n    the handles it needs, avoiding file-descriptor corruption.\n    \"\"\""

> AGENT

The spec and code already cover all modes — the rule is simply:

> **1 replay → N HDF5 files, one per human player, regardless of game mode.**

| Mode | Players | HDF5 files per replay |
|------|---------|----------------------|
| 1v1 | 2 | 2 |
| 2v2 | 4 | 4 |
| 3v3 | 6 | 6 |
| FFA 3 | 3 | 3 |
| FFA 8 | 8 | 8 |

The spec says "one per human player" — FFA was just used as the example because it's the most illustrative case. The pipeline iterates `human_slots` which contains every human player regardless of game mode.

The only thing that changes per mode is the **outcome label**:
- 1v1: winner=1.0, loser=0.0
- Team (2v2, 3v3): all players on winning team=1.0, losing team=0.0
- FFA: survivor/winner=1.0, everyone else=0.0

That team outcome logic isn't written yet — it needs to read the `team` field from `PlayerSlot` to determine which team won. That's a `build_hdf5_dataset.py` concern (not yet built). Want me to add a note about team outcome resolution to the spec?

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/zh-copilot/docs/specs/2026-04-10-per-player-training-fow.md",
  "old_string": "## Open Questions\n\n- **Disconnections mid-game**: record steps up to disconnect, label as loss.\n- **Draw detection**: ZH has no explicit draw — treat as 0.5 if both players\n  quit within 30s of each other.\n- **Observer slots**: skip entirely — no commands, no training example.\n- **Replay simulation speed**: headless engine must run faster than real-time\n  to make dataset construction tractable (~5-10× target).",
  "new_string": "## Outcome Label by Game Mode\n\nThe rule is identical for all modes: **one HDF5 file per human player,\nregardless of game mode.** Only outcome assignment differs.\n\n| Mode | Players | HDF5 files | Winner label | Loser label |\n|------|---------|------------|-------------|-------------|\n| 1v1 | 2 | 2 | 1.0 | 0.0 |\n| 2v2 | 4 | 4 | 1.0 (whole team) | 0.0 (whole team) |\n| 3v3 | 6 | 6 | 1.0 (whole team) | 0.0 (whole team) |\n| FFA 3 | 3 | 3 | 1.0 (last standing) | 0.0 |\n| FFA 8 | 8 | 8 | 1.0 (last standing) | 0.0 |\n\n### Outcome Resolution Logic (build_hdf5_dataset.py)\n\nWin/loss is determined from the replay header + engine state, in priority order:\n\n1. **`replay.header.quit_early`** — if False, last player/team alive wins.\n2. **Team field** — `PlayerSlot.team` (integer, 0=no team / FFA). Players\n   sharing a non-zero team ID are allies; if all enemies are eliminated, that\n   team wins → all members get `outcome=1.0`.\n3. **FFA** (all players have `team=0`) — last player whose buildings are not\n   all destroyed wins → `outcome=1.0`, all others `outcome=0.0`.\n4. **Disconnections** — player who disconnected gets `outcome=0.0`; steps are\n   recorded up to their disconnect frame only.\n5. **Ambiguous / `quit_early`** — all players get `outcome=0.5`.\n\n```python\ndef resolve_outcomes(slots: list[PlayerSlot], winner_team: int | None) -> dict[int, float]:\n    \"\"\"Return {slot_number: outcome} for all human slots.\n\n    winner_team: the team index that won (from engine), or None if ambiguous.\n    Team 0 = FFA (no allies); in FFA, pass the winning slot_number as a\n    special case handled by the caller.\n    \"\"\"\n    outcomes = {}\n    for slot in slots:\n        if not slot.is_human:\n            continue\n        if winner_team is None:\n            outcomes[slot.slot_number] = 0.5  # ambiguous\n        elif slot.team == 0:\n            # FFA — caller sets winner explicitly\n            outcomes[slot.slot_number] = 0.0\n        else:\n            outcomes[slot.slot_number] = 1.0 if slot.team == winner_team else 0.0\n    return outcomes\n```\n\n---\n\n## Open Questions\n\n- **Disconnections mid-game**: record steps up to disconnect frame, label as loss.\n- **Draw detection**: ZH has no explicit draw — treat as 0.5 if `quit_early=True`\n  and no clear winner can be determined from engine state.\n- **Observer slots**: skip entirely — no commands, no training example.\n- **Replay simulation speed**: headless engine must run faster than real-time\n  to make dataset construction tractable (~5-10× target)."
}

158:    SELF_DESTRUCT            = 1093  # player forfeits/quits
166:    # ── Terminal record (outside 1000–1999, signals end of stream) ───────────
215:    MsgType.EXIT:                              "garrison",

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/zh-copilot/src/zh_copilot/replay/analysis.py",
  "old_string": "def filter_replays(replays: list[ReplayFile], min_duration: float = MIN_DURATION_SECONDS, min_commands: int = MIN_COMMANDS) -> list[ReplayFile]:\n    return [r for r in replays if is_valid_for_training(r, min_duration, min_commands)]",
  "new_string": "def filter_replays(replays: list[ReplayFile], min_duration: float = MIN_DURATION_SECONDS, min_commands: int = MIN_COMMANDS) -> list[ReplayFile]:\n    return [r for r in replays if is_valid_for_training(r, min_duration, min_commands)]\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Per-player outcome inference from command stream\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n_INF = float(\"inf\")\n# Two players quitting within this many frames are considered a simultaneous\n# quit (draw). 150 frames = 5 seconds at 30 fps.\n_DRAW_FRAMES = 150\n\n\ndef infer_player_outcomes(replay: ReplayFile, slots: list) -> dict[int, float]:\n    \"\"\"Infer win/loss/draw outcome for each human slot without the game engine.\n\n    Uses SELF_DESTRUCT commands (player quit/forfeit) and player_discons flags\n    from the replay header. Works for all game modes: 1v1, 2v2, 3v3, FFA.\n\n    Returns {slot_number: outcome} where outcome is 1.0=win, 0.0=loss, 0.5=draw/unknown.\n\n    Algorithm:\n      - Disconnected players → quit at frame 0 (loss).\n      - SELF_DESTRUCT command → player forfeited at that frame.\n      - In team games (team > 0): earliest-quitting team loses, other team wins.\n      - In FFA / 1v1 (team == 0): last player standing (quit latest / never quit) wins.\n      - Two players quitting within _DRAW_FRAMES of each other → draw (0.5).\n    \"\"\"\n    # Collect first SELF_DESTRUCT frame per player_idx\n    quit_frames_by_pidx: dict[int, int] = {}\n    for cmd in replay.commands:\n        if cmd.msg_type == MsgType.SELF_DESTRUCT and cmd.player_idx not in quit_frames_by_pidx:\n            quit_frames_by_pidx[cmd.player_idx] = cmd.frame\n\n    human_slots = [s for s in slots if s.is_human]\n    if not human_slots:\n        return {}\n\n    # Per-slot quit frame (inf = never quit = still standing)\n    slot_quit: dict[int, float] = {}\n    for slot in human_slots:\n        if replay.header.player_discons[slot.slot_number]:\n            slot_quit[slot.slot_number] = 0.0  # disconnected = quit immediately\n        elif slot.player_idx in quit_frames_by_pidx:\n            slot_quit[slot.slot_number] = float(quit_frames_by_pidx[slot.player_idx])\n        else:\n            slot_quit[slot.slot_number] = _INF  # never quit\n\n    is_team_game = any(s.team > 0 for s in human_slots)\n\n    if is_team_game:\n        # Group slot numbers by team id\n        teams: dict[int, list[int]] = {}\n        for slot in human_slots:\n            teams.setdefault(slot.team, []).append(slot.slot_number)\n\n        # Each team's \"loss frame\" = earliest quit among any member\n        team_loss_frame: dict[int, float] = {\n            team: min(slot_quit[s] for s in slot_nums)\n            for team, slot_nums in teams.items()\n        }\n\n        # Team that lost first\n        losing_team = min(team_loss_frame, key=team_loss_frame.__getitem__)\n        losing_frame = team_loss_frame[losing_team]\n\n        if losing_frame == _INF:\n            # Nobody quit — ambiguous\n            return {s.slot_number: 0.5 for s in human_slots}\n\n        outcomes: dict[int, float] = {}\n        for slot in human_slots:\n            if slot.team == losing_team:\n                outcomes[slot.slot_number] = 0.0\n            else:\n                # Win, unless their team also quit within draw threshold\n                own_frame = team_loss_frame[slot.team]\n                if own_frame - losing_frame <= _DRAW_FRAMES:\n                    outcomes[slot.slot_number] = 0.5\n                else:\n                    outcomes[slot.slot_number] = 1.0\n        return outcomes\n\n    else:\n        # 1v1 or FFA — winner is last player standing\n        never_quit = [sn for sn, f in slot_quit.items() if f == _INF]\n\n        if len(never_quit) == 1:\n            return {s.slot_number: (1.0 if s.slot_number == never_quit[0] else 0.0) for s in human_slots}\n\n        if len(never_quit) > 1:\n            # Multiple players never quit — ambiguous\n            return {s.slot_number: (0.5 if s.slot_number in never_quit else 0.0) for s in human_slots}\n\n        # All players quit — winner is whoever quit last\n        sorted_by_frame = sorted(slot_quit.items(), key=lambda x: x[1])\n        last_sn, last_frame = sorted_by_frame[-1]\n        prev_frame = sorted_by_frame[-2][1] if len(sorted_by_frame) > 1 else 0.0\n\n        outcomes = {}\n        for slot in human_slots:\n            f = slot_quit[slot.slot_number]\n            if f == last_frame:\n                outcomes[slot.slot_number] = 0.5 if (last_frame - prev_frame) <= _DRAW_FRAMES else 1.0\n            else:\n                outcomes[slot.slot_number] = 0.0\n        return outcomes"
}

Done. Per-player outcomes stored across all 36k replays:

- **43,798 losses** (0.0), **29,283 wins** (1.0), **14,756 draws/ambiguous** (0.5)
- Win rate ~33% is expected — many multi-player games (FFA, team) have more losers than winners per replay
- 16.8% draws = games with no clear `SELF_DESTRUCT` signal (timeout, connection drop without flag, etc.) — these still train but the outcome signal is weaker

Each player record in `players[]` now has: `slot`, `player_idx`, `name`, `team`, `general`, `faction`, `outcome`. When `build_hdf5_dataset.py` is written in Phase 2, it reads straight from this — no outcome logic needed there.

Game options S= field:
S=H^^,4FD6718B,11813,TT,4,-1,1,-1,1:Hi_go_there====>{0},4F658E3A,29900,TT,-1,-1,-1,-1,1:X:X:X:X:X:X:
Slots: [(0, -1), (1, -1)]
```

> AGENT

It's the raw value from the ZH game options string — the 8th field in the slot descriptor. In the lobby, when players don't set a team (1v1 or FFA), the game stores `-1` meaning "no team assigned."

ZH team values:
- `-1` = no team (1v1 or FFA — players are fighting each other individually)
- `0` = also seen in some FFA lobbies (same meaning, different encoding)
- `1`, `2`, ... = explicit team groups (2v2, 3v3, etc.)

The outcome inference correctly handles it — `is_team_game = any(s.team > 0 ...)` so `-1` and `0` both route to the FFA/1v1 branch. The label is accurate.

1	# src/zh_copilot/training/strategy_cluster.py
2	"""Strategy archetype clustering via K-means on opening command frequencies."""
3	
4	from __future__ import annotations
5	
6	import pickle
7	from pathlib import Path
8	from typing import Sequence
9	
10	import h5py
11	import numpy as np
12	from sklearn.cluster import KMeans
13	
14	from zh_copilot.config import ENGINE, MODEL
15	
16	# 5-minute opening window in game logic frames
17	_DEFAULT_MAX_FRAMES: int = ENGINE.logic_fps * 60 * 5  # 9000
18	
19	
20	def extract_opening_features(
21	    path: str | Path,
22	    num_action_types: int = MODEL.num_action_types,
23	    max_frames: int = _DEFAULT_MAX_FRAMES,
24	) -> np.ndarray:
25	    """Return an L2-normalised action-type frequency histogram for a replay's opening.
26	
27	    Only steps whose ``frame`` value is strictly less than ``max_frames`` are
28	    counted.  If no steps qualify (empty file, max_frames=0, or all frames beyond
29	    the window), a zero vector of shape ``(num_action_types,)`` is returned.
30	
31	    Parameters
32	    ----------
33	    path:
34	        Path to an HDF5 replay file.
35	    num_action_types:
36	        Number of bins in the histogram (default: MODEL.num_action_types = 21).
37	    max_frames:
38	        Upper bound (exclusive) on the frame index to include.
39	
40	    Returns
41	    -------
42	    np.ndarray
43	        Shape ``(num_action_types,)`` float32 vector, L2-normalised (or zero).
44	    """
45	    path = Path(path)
46	    with h5py.File(path, "r") as f:
47	        frames = f["frame"][:]          # (T,) int32
48	        action_types = f["action_type"][:]  # (T,) int32
49	
50	    mask = frames < max_frames
51	    qualifying_actions = action_types[mask]
52	
53	    hist = np.zeros(num_action_types, dtype=np.float32)
54	    if qualifying_actions.size == 0:
55	        return hist
56	
57	    for a in qualifying_actions:
58	        if 0 <= a < num_action_types:
59	            hist[a] += 1.0
60	
61	    norm = np.linalg.norm(hist)
62	    if norm > 0.0:
63	        hist /= norm
64	
65	    return hist
66	
67	
68	def fit_strategy_clusters(
69	    file_paths: Sequence[str | Path],
70	    n_clusters: int = MODEL.num_strategy_archetypes,
71	    max_frames: int = _DEFAULT_MAX_FRAMES,
72	    seed: int = 42,
73	) -> list[int]:
74	    """Fit K-means on opening features extracted from each replay file.
75	
76	    If the number of files is smaller than ``n_clusters``, ``n_clusters`` is
77	    capped to ``len(file_paths)`` so K-means does not raise.
78	
79	    Parameters
80	    ----------
81	    file_paths:
82	        Sequence of paths to HDF5 replay files.
83	    n_clusters:
84	        Desired number of strategy archetypes.
85	    max_frames:
86	        Passed through to :func:`extract_opening_features`.
87	    seed:
88	        Random seed for reproducibility.
89	
90	    Returns
91	    -------
92	    list[int]
93	        Cluster label (int) for each replay file, in the same order.
94	    """
95	    file_paths = [Path(p) for p in file_paths]
96	    features = np.stack(
97	        [extract_opening_features(p, max_frames=max_frames) for p in file_paths],
98	        axis=0,
99	    )  # (N, num_action_types)
100	
101	    effective_k = min(n_clusters, len(file_paths))
102	    km = KMeans(n_clusters=effective_k, random_state=seed, n_init="auto")
103	    km.fit(features)
104	    return [int(lbl) for lbl in km.labels_]
105	
106	
107	def label_replay_files(
108	    file_paths: Sequence[str | Path],
109	    labels: Sequence[int],
110	) -> None:
111	    """Write strategy labels into each HDF5 file.
112	
113	    For each file, two things are written:
114	    - ``f.attrs["strategy_label"]`` — scalar int attribute.
115	    - ``f["strategy_label_steps"]`` — 1-D int32 dataset of length T, filled with
116	      the label value (one entry per timestep), matching the convention used by
117	      ``make_synthetic_h5`` and expected by ``StratifiedBatchSampler``.
118	
119	    Existing datasets are deleted and re-created (overwrite semantics).
120	
121	    Parameters
122	    ----------
123	    file_paths:
124	        Sequence of HDF5 replay file paths.
125	    labels:
126	        Integer label for each file (same order as ``file_paths``).
127	    """
128	    for path, lbl in zip(file_paths, labels):
129	        path = Path(path)
130	        lbl_int = int(lbl)
131	        with h5py.File(path, "r+") as f:
132	            num_steps = f["action_type"].shape[0]
133	
134	            # Scalar attribute (strategy_label)
135	            f.attrs["strategy_label"] = lbl_int
136	
137	            # Per-step dataset (strategy_label_steps)
138	            if "strategy_label_steps" in f:
139	                del f["strategy_label_steps"]
140	            f.create_dataset(
141	                "strategy_label_steps",
142	                data=np.full(num_steps, lbl_int, dtype=np.int32),
143	            )
144	
145	
146	class StrategyClusterer:
147	    """Convenience wrapper around :func:`fit_strategy_clusters` and
148	    :func:`label_replay_files` with save/load support via pickle.
149	
150	    Parameters
151	    ----------
152	    n_clusters:
153	        Number of strategy archetypes for K-means.
154	    max_frames:
155	        Opening window length in game frames (default: 9000 = 5 minutes @ 30 fps).
156	    seed:
157	        Random seed for K-means reproducibility.
158	    """
159	
160	    def __init__(
161	        self,
162	        n_clusters: int = MODEL.num_strategy_archetypes,
163	        max_frames: int = _DEFAULT_MAX_FRAMES,
164	        seed: int = 42,
165	    ) -> None:
166	        self.n_clusters = n_clusters
167	        self.max_frames = max_frames
168	        self.seed = seed
169	        self._km: KMeans | None = None
170	
171	    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
172	    # Public API
173	    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
174	
175	    def fit(self, file_paths: Sequence[str | Path]) -> "StrategyClusterer":
176	        """Fit K-means on opening features extracted from ``file_paths``.
177	
178	        Parameters
179	        ----------
180	        file_paths:
181	            HDF5 replay files to cluster.
182	
183	        Returns
184	        -------
185	        self
186	        """
187	        file_paths = [Path(p) for p in file_paths]
188	        features = np.stack(
189	            [
190	                extract_opening_features(p, max_frames=self.max_frames)
191	                for p in file_paths
192	            ],
193	            axis=0,
194	        )
195	        effective_k = min(self.n_clusters, len(file_paths))
196	        self._km = KMeans(n_clusters=effective_k, random_state=self.seed, n_init="auto")
197	        self._km.fit(features)
198	        return self
199	
200	    def predict(self, file_paths: Sequence[str | Path]) -> list[int]:
201	        """Predict strategy labels for ``file_paths`` using the fitted model.
202	
203	        Parameters
204	        ----------
205	        file_paths:
206	            HDF5 replay files to label.
207	
208	        Returns
209	        -------
210	        list[int]
211	            Cluster label for each file.
212	
213	        Raises
214	        ------
215	        RuntimeError
216	            If ``fit`` has not been called yet.
217	        """
218	        if self._km is None:
219	            raise RuntimeError("StrategyClusterer is not fitted — call fit() first.")
220	
221	        file_paths = [Path(p) for p in file_paths]
222	        features = np.stack(
223	            [
224	                extract_opening_features(p, max_frames=self.max_frames)
225	                for p in file_paths
226	            ],
227	            axis=0,
228	        )
229	        return [int(lbl) for lbl in self._km.predict(features)]
230	
231	    def save(self, path: str | Path) -> None:
232	        """Pickle the clusterer to ``path``.
233	
234	        Parameters
235	        ----------
236	        path:
237	            Destination file path (will be created/overwritten).
238	        """
239	        path = Path(path)
240	        path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
241	        with open(path, "wb") as fh:
242	            pickle.dump(self, fh)
243	
244	    @classmethod
245	    def load(cls, path: str | Path) -> "StrategyClusterer":
246	        """Load a pickled :class:`StrategyClusterer` from ``path``.
247	
248	        Parameters
249	        ----------
250	        path:
251	            Path to the pickle file created by :meth:`save`.
252	
253	        Returns
254	        -------
255	        StrategyClusterer
256	        """
257	        with open(Path(path), "rb") as fh:
258	            obj = pickle.load(fh)
259	        if not isinstance(obj, cls):
260	            raise TypeError(f"Expected StrategyClusterer, got {type(obj)}")
261	        return obj
262	
```

> AGENT

It's designed for the wrong stage. It runs **K-means on the first 5 minutes of HDF5 files** (action-type frequency histograms) to cluster replays into 20 "strategy archetypes" — e.g. rush, turtle, mass-air, etc.

The problem: **it needs HDF5 files to run**, which don't exist yet (Phase 1 blocker). It reads `frame` and `action_type` datasets from HDF5.

So `strategy_label` can't be generated from the current `.rep` metadata alone. The clustering runs after HDF5 construction, not before.

The correct order is:
```
replays/*.rep
    ↓ Phase 1
HDF5 files (per-player, FOW-masked)
    ↓ strategy_cluster.py
strategy_label written into each HDF5 + metadata
    ↓
ILTrainer with StratifiedBatchSampler
```

**Bottom line:** metadata is as complete as it can be right now. There's nothing more to add pre-Phase 1. The only two things blocking training are both gated on the headless engine.

> DEVELOPER

ЦРфе ші зрфіу 1

> AGENT

Phase 1 is building the **headless game engine** — running Zero Hour without a screen, purely for simulation.

ZH is a 2003 Windows DirectX game. To use it for training you need it to run on a server/Mac with no display, no sound, faster than real-time. That requires stripping out all the rendering code and replacing it with stubs.

**What needs to happen:**

1. **Strip DirectX/rendering** — `GameEngineDevice`, `W3DDisplay`, audio — replace with null stubs so it compiles without a GPU/display
2. **Compile on Intel Mac** — the EA source is x86 C++, only builds on Intel architecture
3. **Game state extraction (C++)** — at each tick, read out: unit positions, health, money, fog-of-war visibility grid per player, buildings, etc.
4. **Action injection (C++)** — feed commands into the engine the same way the network does in multiplayer (same format as `.rep` command stream)
5. **pybind11 bindings** — expose state extraction and action injection to Python as numpy arrays
6. **Replay simulation** — feed a `.rep` file's commands into the engine tick-by-tick, pause at each decision point, extract state → write to HDF5

**Why it's hard:**

- The source code compiles on Windows with MSVC. Getting it to compile on macOS with clang requires patching platform-specific code (Win32 APIs, DirectX headers, COM interfaces)
- The rendering and game logic are entangled — separating them without breaking the simulation requires careful surgery
- No documentation — pure reverse engineering from the source

**It gates everything downstream:** HDF5 dataset, strategy clustering, IL training evaluation, all of RL. Nothing trains until this exists.

Is this something you're planning to tackle on the Intel Mac soon?

> DEVELOPER

a few simple question.. is it possible to spin up virtual machine locally for it? we are on m4 mac

> AGENT

Yes, but with an important caveat.

**What works on M4 VM:**
- Parallels or UTM can run Windows ARM natively — fast
- Windows ARM has built-in x86 emulation, so MSVC and ZH's x86 code will run
- Fine for **compilation and development**

**The problem — speed:**
- x86 emulation on ARM is roughly 30-50% of native x86 speed
- Dataset generation needs the engine running **5-10x faster than real-time**
- Emulated x86 on M4 will likely run at **0.5-1x real-time** — too slow
- 36k replays × ~2.5 players = ~90k sequences to simulate. At 1x real-time that's months.

**Practical split:**

| Task | Where |
|------|--------|
| Compile headless engine + develop | Parallels on M4 (x86 emulation, one-time) |
| Generate HDF5 dataset (36k replays) | Cloud x86 Windows VM — AWS/GCP spot instance, native speed, cheap |
| Python training (PyTorch) | M4 Mac natively (MPS) |

**Bottom line:** use Parallels on M4 to get Phase 1 unblocked and working. When it's time to generate the full dataset, spin up a cheap cloud x86 instance for the heavy simulation job. You pay for maybe a few hours of compute, not ongoing.

Parallels is the fastest path to unblocking Phase 1 right now.

**Windows Installation for Claude Code — Short Version:**

**Does NOT require WSL.** Claude Code runs natively on Windows.

**Install steps:**

1. **Install Git for Windows first** (required): https://git-scm.com/downloads/win

2. **Choose your method:**
   - **PowerShell** (recommended):
     ```powershell
     irm https://claude.ai/install.ps1 | iex
     ```
   - **CMD**:
     ```batch
     curl -fsSL https://claude.ai/install.cmd -o install.cmd && install.cmd && del install.cmd
     ```
   - **WinGet**:
     ```powershell
     winget install Anthropic.ClaudeCode
     ```

3. **Log in**:
   ```bash
   claude
   /login
   ```

4. **Start coding**:
   ```bash
   claude
   ```

**Notes:**
- Native install auto-updates (PowerShell/CMD versions)
- WinGet doesn't auto-update — run `winget upgrade Anthropic.ClaudeCode` periodically
- Homebrew also available but doesn't auto-update

That's it. No WSL needed.

> DEVELOPER

question... why do we need strategy_cluster.py? for what?

> AGENT

It's for **balanced training batches**, not for the model's intelligence.

The problem it solves: without it, the `StratifiedBatchSampler` would draw random batches that might be 80% China Tank rush replays and 20% everything else — because rush is the most common playstyle. The model would overfit to one strategy.

`strategy_cluster.py` looks at the **first 5 minutes of each replay** (action-type frequency histogram) and runs K-means to group replays into 20 archetypes:
- Cluster 0 = "mass units early, aggressive"
- Cluster 7 = "turtle, build defenses, tech up"
- Cluster 14 = "fast expand, economy focus"
- etc. (labels are learned, not named)

Then the sampler ensures each training batch has **balanced representation** across strategies, factions, and game phases — so the model sees diverse playstyles equally.

**The honest assessment though:** it's premature optimization. The clusters are based on action-type frequencies which are very coarse (21 action types). Real strategy variety is much richer. It's a reasonable approximation but not critical for getting training started.

**Priority:** low. The hard blocker is Phase 1. Strategy clustering is a training quality improvement that can be added after the first training runs show imbalance problems.

> DEVELOPER

shiw me all strategies

> AGENT

There are no defined strategies — they don't exist yet.

`strategy_cluster.py` **learns** them from data via K-means. The 20 clusters are unnamed integer labels (0–19) that emerge from the first 5 minutes of gameplay patterns. Nobody predefines what they mean — you'd have to inspect the cluster centroids after running it to understand what each one represents.

And it can't run yet — needs HDF5 files (Phase 1 blocker).

If you want **human-defined strategies** instead of learned ones, that's a different approach — you'd manually label archetypes like:

- Rush (attack before 5 min)
- Tech (fast tier-3 units)
- Turtle (mass defenses)
- Eco (supply/resources focused)
- Superweapon race
- etc.

That would be more interpretable and could be derived from replay metadata we already have (build order timing, first attack frame, resource efficiency). Want to go that route instead?

> DEVELOPER

i think we dont need it...

> AGENT

Agreed. Remove it.

The `StratifiedBatchSampler` can stratify by `faction_id` + `game_phase` + `outcome` — all of which come naturally from the per-player HDF5 metadata. That's meaningful stratification without needing learned clusters.

# src/zh_copilot/training/strategy_sampler.py
"""StratifiedBatchSampler — balanced batches by (faction_id, strategy_label, game_phase)."""

from __future__ import annotations

import random
from collections import defaultdict
from typing import Iterator, List, Sequence, Tuple

from torch.utils.data import Sampler

StratumKey = Tuple[int, int, int]


class StratifiedBatchSampler(Sampler[List[int]]):
    """Yield batches with capped per-stratum contribution for balanced IL training.

    Groups dataset indices by ``(faction_id, strategy_label, game_phase)`` and
    fills each batch using round-robin across strata, capping any single stratum
    at ``int(max_same_stratum * batch_size)`` slots (minimum 1).

    When only one stratum exists the cap is overridden to ``batch_size`` so
    batches can still be filled.

    Args:
        labels: One ``(faction_id, strategy_label, game_phase)`` tuple per index.
        batch_size: Number of samples per batch.
        max_same_stratum: Maximum fraction any one stratum may occupy (default 0.4).
        drop_last: Drop the final incomplete batch (default ``True``).
        shuffle: Shuffle indices within each stratum before iterating (default ``True``).
        seed: Seed for ``random.Random`` — ensures reproducibility across epochs.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        labels: Sequence[StratumKey],
        batch_size: int,
        max_same_stratum: float = 0.4,
        drop_last: bool = True,
        shuffle: bool = True,
        seed: int = 0,
    ) -> None:
        self.labels = list(labels)
        self.batch_size = batch_size
        self.max_same_stratum = max_same_stratum
        self.drop_last = drop_last
        self.shuffle = shuffle
        self.seed = seed

        # Build stratum -> [indices] mapping (fixed; shuffled each __iter__)
        self._strata: dict[StratumKey, List[int]] = defaultdict(list)
        for idx, key in enumerate(self.labels):
            self._strata[key].append(idx)
        self._strata_keys: List[StratumKey] = list(self._strata.keys())

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Core iteration
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def __iter__(self) -> Iterator[List[int]]:
        rng = random.Random(self.seed)

        n_strata = len(self._strata_keys)
        # Per-stratum cap; override to batch_size when there is only one stratum.
        if n_strata == 1:
            per_stratum_cap = self.batch_size
        else:
            per_stratum_cap = max(1, int(self.max_same_stratum * self.batch_size))

        # Shuffle each stratum's indices independently
        pools: dict[StratumKey, List[int]] = {}
        for key in self._strata_keys:
            indices = list(self._strata[key])
            if self.shuffle:
                rng.shuffle(indices)
            pools[key] = indices

        # Pointers into each stratum pool
        pointers: dict[StratumKey, int] = {k: 0 for k in self._strata_keys}

        def _remaining(key: StratumKey) -> int:
            return len(pools[key]) - pointers[key]

        batches: List[List[int]] = []
        current_batch: List[int] = []

        # Round-robin across strata until all pools are exhausted
        active_keys = list(self._strata_keys)
        rng.shuffle(active_keys)  # randomise stratum order each epoch

        while True:
            # Refresh active keys — keep only strata that still have indices
            active_keys = [k for k in active_keys if _remaining(k) > 0]
            if not active_keys:
                break

            # One round: each stratum contributes up to per_stratum_cap items
            for key in list(active_keys):
                slots_left_in_batch = self.batch_size - len(current_batch)
                if slots_left_in_batch == 0:
                    batches.append(current_batch)
                    current_batch = []
                    slots_left_in_batch = self.batch_size

                take = min(per_stratum_cap, slots_left_in_batch, _remaining(key))
                ptr = pointers[key]
                current_batch.extend(pools[key][ptr: ptr + take])
                pointers[key] = ptr + take

                if len(current_batch) == self.batch_size:
                    batches.append(current_batch)
                    current_batch = []

        # Handle leftover items in current_batch
        if current_batch:
            if not self.drop_last:
                batches.append(current_batch)
            # else discard

        # Shuffle batch order for extra randomness
        rng.shuffle(batches)
        yield from batches

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Length
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def __len__(self) -> int:
        """Return number of batches, computed by simulating one iteration."""
        return sum(1 for _ in self)

# src/zh_copilot/training/il_trainer.py
"""ILTrainer — behaviour-cloning training loop for ZHCopilotModel.

Features:
- StratifiedBatchSampler for training (prevents batch composition bias)
- AdamW optimiser
- CosineAnnealingLR scheduler (set up in train() once total epochs are known)
- Gradient clipping (max_norm=1.0)
- TensorBoard logging to <output_dir>/logs/
- Checkpoint save/load to <output_dir>/checkpoint_epoch_NNN.pt
- LSTM hidden state reset each forward pass (correct for shuffled IL batches)
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

import h5py
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
from torch.optim import AdamW
from torch.optim.lr_scheduler import CosineAnnealingLR
from torch.utils.data import DataLoader
from torch.utils.tensorboard import SummaryWriter

from zh_copilot.training.il_dataset import ILDataset
from zh_copilot.training.strategy_sampler import StratifiedBatchSampler
from zh_copilot.training.losses import il_loss

# Map action type index -> human-readable category name
_ACTION_CATEGORIES: dict[int, str] = {
    0: "move", 1: "attack", 2: "build", 3: "produce", 4: "research",
    5: "sell", 6: "rally", 7: "general_power", 8: "ability", 9: "guard",
    10: "stop", 11: "garrison", 12: "select", 13: "repair", 14: "enter",
    15: "capture", 16: "dock", 17: "evacuate", 18: "special", 19: "other",
    20: "noop",
}

# Keys from batch that the model actually needs
_MODEL_KEYS = {
    "spatial",
    "entity_features",
    "entity_mask",
    "global_scalars",
    "strategy_override",
}


def _build_stratum_labels(dataset: ILDataset) -> list[tuple[int, int, int]]:
    """Extract (faction_id, strategy_label, game_phase) tuples cheaply from HDF5.

    Reads directly from HDF5 arrays — no PyTorch tensor allocation, no
    dataset __getitem__ calls.
    """
    labels: list[tuple[int, int, int]] = []
    for fp in dataset.file_paths:
        with h5py.File(fp, "r") as f:
            n = int(f.attrs.get("num_steps", len(f["action_type"])))
            faction = (
                f["faction_id"][:n].astype(int).tolist()
                if "faction_id" in f
                else [0] * n
            )
            strategy = (
                f["strategy_label"][:n].astype(int).tolist()
                if "strategy_label" in f
                else [0] * n
            )
            phase = (
                f["game_phase"][:n].astype(int).tolist()
                if "game_phase" in f
                else [0] * n
            )
            labels.extend(zip(faction, strategy, phase))
    return labels


def _model_batch(batch: dict[str, torch.Tensor], device: torch.device) -> dict[str, torch.Tensor]:
    """Filter and move only the keys the model needs to device."""

# tests/training/test_strategy_sampler.py
"""Tests for StratifiedBatchSampler."""

from __future__ import annotations

import pytest
import torch
from torch.utils.data import DataLoader, TensorDataset
from collections import Counter

from zh_copilot.training.strategy_sampler import StratifiedBatchSampler


def make_labels(n: int, n_factions: int = 3, n_strats: int = 4, n_phases: int = 3, seed: int = 0):
    """Generate random (faction_id, strategy_label, game_phase) label tuples."""
    import random
    random.seed(seed)
    return [
        (random.randint(0, n_factions - 1),
         random.randint(0, n_strats - 1),
         random.randint(0, n_phases - 1))
        for _ in range(n)
    ]


def test_batch_size_correct():
    """All batches (with drop_last=True) must have exactly batch_size items."""
    labels = make_labels(200, seed=1)
    sampler = StratifiedBatchSampler(labels, batch_size=16, drop_last=True)
    for batch in sampler:
        assert len(batch) == 16


def test_no_stratum_exceeds_cap():
    """No stratum should contribute more than max_same_stratum * batch_size per batch."""
    labels = make_labels(500, n_factions=2, n_strats=2, n_phases=2, seed=2)
    batch_size = 32
    max_frac = 0.4
    sampler = StratifiedBatchSampler(labels, batch_size=batch_size, max_same_stratum=max_frac, drop_last=True)
    cap = int(max_frac * batch_size) + 1  # +1 for rounding tolerance

48

50	def _build_stratum_labels(dataset: ILDataset) -> list[tuple[int, int, int]]:
51	    """Extract (faction_id, strategy_label, game_phase) tuples cheaply from HDF5.
52	
53	    Reads directly from HDF5 arrays — no PyTorch tensor allocation, no
54	    dataset __getitem__ calls.
55	    """
56	    labels: list[tuple[int, int, int]] = []
57	    for fp in dataset.file_paths:
58	        with h5py.File(fp, "r") as f:
59	            n = int(f.attrs.get("num_steps", len(f["action_type"])))
60	            faction = (
61	                f["faction_id"][:n].astype(int).tolist()
62	                if "faction_id" in f
63	                else [0] * n
64	            )
65	            strategy = (
66	                f["strategy_label"][:n].astype(int).tolist()
67	                if "strategy_label" in f
68	                else [0] * n
69	            )
70	            phase = (
71	                f["game_phase"][:n].astype(int).tolist()
72	                if "game_phase" in f
73	                else [0] * n
74	            )
75	            labels.extend(zip(faction, strategy, phase))
76	    return labels
77	
```

  "old_string": "def _build_stratum_labels(dataset: ILDataset) -> list[tuple[int, int, int]]:\n    \"\"\"Extract (faction_id, strategy_label, game_phase) tuples cheaply from HDF5.\n\n    Reads directly from HDF5 arrays — no PyTorch tensor allocation, no\n    dataset __getitem__ calls.\n    \"\"\"\n    labels: list[tuple[int, int, int]] = []\n    for fp in dataset.file_paths:\n        with h5py.File(fp, \"r\") as f:\n            n = int(f.attrs.get(\"num_steps\", len(f[\"action_type\"])))\n            faction = (\n                f[\"faction_id\"][:n].astype(int).tolist()\n                if \"faction_id\" in f\n                else [0] * n\n            )\n            strategy = (\n                f[\"strategy_label\"][:n].astype(int).tolist()\n                if \"strategy_label\" in f\n                else [0] * n\n            )\n            phase = (\n                f[\"game_phase\"][:n].astype(int).tolist()\n                if \"game_phase\" in f\n                else [0] * n\n            )\n            labels.extend(zip(faction, strategy, phase))\n    return labels",
  "new_string": "def _build_stratum_labels(dataset: ILDataset) -> list[tuple[int, int]]:\n    \"\"\"Extract (general_id, game_phase) tuples cheaply from HDF5.\n\n    Reads directly from HDF5 arrays — no PyTorch tensor allocation, no\n    dataset __getitem__ calls.\n    \"\"\"\n    labels: list[tuple[int, int]] = []\n    for fp in dataset.file_paths:\n        with h5py.File(fp, \"r\") as f:\n            n = int(f.attrs.get(\"num_steps\", len(f[\"action_type\"])))\n            general = (\n                f[\"general_id\"][:n].astype(int).tolist()\n                if \"general_id\" in f\n                else [0] * n\n            )\n            phase = (\n                f[\"game_phase\"][:n].astype(int).tolist()\n                if \"game_phase\" in f\n                else [0] * n\n            )\n            labels.extend(zip(general, phase))\n    return labels"
}

134	def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
135	    parser = build_parser()
136	    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
137	
138	    # ---- Locate HDF5 files ------------------------------------------------
139	    data_dir = args.data_dir.resolve()
140	    if not data_dir.is_dir():
141	        print(f"ERROR: --data-dir '{data_dir}' is not a directory.", file=sys.stderr)
142	        return 1
143	
144	    file_paths = sorted(data_dir.glob("*.h5"))
145	    if not file_paths:
146	        print(f"ERROR: No .h5 files found in '{data_dir}'.", file=sys.stderr)
147	        return 1
148	
149	    print(f"Found {len(file_paths)} replay file(s) in {data_dir}")
150	
151	    # ---- Optional strategy clustering -------------------------------------
152	    if args.cluster_strategies:
153	        print(f"Running StrategyClusterer with n_clusters={args.n_clusters} ...")
154	        clusterer = StrategyClusterer(n_clusters=args.n_clusters, seed=args.seed)
155	        clusterer.fit(file_paths)
156	        labels = clusterer.predict(file_paths)
157	        label_replay_files(file_paths, labels)
158	        # Save clusterer alongside checkpoints
159	        args.output_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
160	        clusterer.save(args.output_dir / "strategy_clusterer.pkl")
161	        print("Strategy labels written to HDF5 files and clusterer saved.")
162	
163	    # ---- Train / val split ------------------------------------------------
164	    train_dataset, val_dataset = ILDataset.split(
165	        file_paths,
166	        val_fraction=args.val_fraction,
167	        shuffle=True,
168	        seed=args.seed,
169	    )
